I just have to hear them saying those awful things while I'm trying to get through the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior or daily activities since the voices started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. I don't really know why I do it, but it's definitely been happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected or don't make sense when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that problem. rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: I’ve gone many nights sleeping maybe two or three hours, then getting up feeling charged like I had a full rest and going straight back to painting or making calls.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My thoughts run so fast that one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song, and I can’t slow it down enough to make sense.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I’ll try to finish a care note or a crochet row, but a car door outside, a bird at the window, or a color on the table pulls me away and I’m off doing something else before I know it.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was sleeping only a few hours and pushing my big art-and-care plans, I was also hearing the voices in the studio and feeling certain people were watching my work.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this stretch, I was either wound up with big plans, little sleep, and nonstop activity, or coming down from that but still not back to myself.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a time after my energy dropped and I was sleeping more normally when the voices and the belief that people were judging or tracking my work kept going for several weeks.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I got to the point where my aide job was in danger because I missed shifts, mixed up care notes, and could not keep visits organized the way I used to. They're pretty much always around in some way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble understanding what's real and what's not, aside from the voices you hear?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I do. Like... I got it in my head that the other home-care agency in town, and even some people down at the library, were watching me. I just believed they were keeping an eye on my paintings and my client papers, trying to steal a chance that was meant for me. Folks tried to tell me it made absolutely no sense, but... I still knew they were doing it. It's just really starting to bother me.\n\nDoctor: What kind of things do the voices say about your work?\n\nPatient: They just... they tell me I'm doing a terrible job. I work as a personal care aide, you know, helping people out. And the voices, they'll say things like I'm making mistakes, or that the people I'm caring for are in danger because of me. It's just a constant criticism, telling me I'm not good enough and that everyone is watching me fail.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to express yourself or keep track of your thoughts when these voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I can still think straight, mostly. It's just... it's hard to ignore them, you know? But I don't lose my train of thought or forget what I want to say. I just have to hear them saying those awful things while I'm trying to get through the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior or daily activities since the voices started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. There have been a few times lately on my client visits where I'll just... stop what I'm supposed to be doing. Or sometimes I'll just stand out in the hallway staring, almost like I'm waiting for some kind of signal before I can move again. It's like I'm bouncing between feeling amazing and being completely on edge.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any periods of time, say a few weeks or more, where you didn't hear the voices at all?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I still knew they were doing it. It felt completely real to me, no matter what anyone said.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think people are watching you or plotting against you when the voices are not talking to you?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said before, the voices don't really ever go away for long. They say my name, and they keep telling me my work is being judged. But the house is completely quiet, and there's nobody else there. It's just really starting to bother me.\n\nDoctor: What kind of things do the voices say about your work?\n\nPatient: They just... they tell me I'm doing a terrible job. I work as a personal care aide, you know, helping people out. And the voices, they'll say things like I'm making mistakes, or that the people I'm caring for are in danger because of me. It's just a constant criticism, telling me I'm not good enough and that everyone is watching me fail.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to express yourself or keep track of your thoughts when these voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I can still think straight, mostly. It's just... it's hard to ignore them, you know? But I don't lose my train of thought or forget what I want to say. I just have to hear them saying those awful things while I'm trying to get through the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior or daily activities since the voices started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. There have been a few times lately on my client visits where I'll just... stop what I'm supposed to be doing. I'll take things like disposable gloves, or those little plastic pill cups, or even just scraps from my crocheting, and I'll start lining them all up in patterns on the table. Or sometimes I'll just stand out in the hallway staring, almost like I'm waiting for some kind of signal before I can move again. I don't really know why I do it, but it's definitely been happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected or don't make sense when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that problem. When it's quiet and the voices aren't there, my thoughts make perfect sense to me. I mean... I know what I'm doing and what I want to say. Everything is pretty clear in my head, they don't feel jumbled or disconnected at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you only line things up in patterns or stand still when the voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: No, it's not just when they're talking. I mean... sometimes it happens when it's quiet, too. I'll just find myself arranging those little cups or standing there stuck in the hall, even if the voices aren't bothering me right then. It doesn't seem to matter if they are yelling at me or not, it just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel extremely happy or euphoric at times for no particular reason?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Sometimes I feel... just so bright and full of energy, like I'm chosen for something special, you know? Like I have all these big plans and this whole town—Greenville—just isn't big enough to hold them all. But then... it can flip. I'll go from feeling on top of the world to just snapping hard at someone over nothing. Like, if one of my clients just asks me to repeat myself, I'll get so irritable and just bite their head off. It's like I'm bouncing between feeling amazing and being completely on edge.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any periods of time, say a few weeks or more, where you didn't hear the voices at all?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I just believed they were keeping an eye on my paintings and my client papers, trying to steal a chance that was meant for me. Folks tried to tell me it made absolutely no sense, but... I still knew they were doing it. It felt completely real to me, no matter what anyone said.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think people are watching you or plotting against you when the voices are not talking to you?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said before, the voices don't really ever go away for long. I don't really separate them in my head like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to make decisions or stick to a plan because your thoughts seem disorganized?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. It's just really starting to bother me.\n\nDoctor: What kind of things do the voices say about your work?\n\nPatient: They just... they tell me I'm doing a terrible job. I work as a personal care aide, you know, helping people out. And the voices, they'll say things like I'm making mistakes, or that the people I'm caring for are in danger because of me. It's just a constant criticism, telling me I'm not good enough and that everyone is watching me fail.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to express yourself or keep track of your thoughts when these voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I can still think straight, mostly. It's just... it's hard to ignore them, you know? But I don't lose my train of thought or forget what I want to say. I just have to hear them saying those awful things while I'm trying to get through the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior or daily activities since the voices started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. There have been a few times lately on my client visits where I'll just... stop what I'm supposed to be doing. I'll take things like disposable gloves, or those little plastic pill cups, or even just scraps from my crocheting, and I'll start lining them all up in patterns on the table. Or sometimes I'll just stand out in the hallway staring, almost like I'm waiting for some kind of signal before I can move again. I don't really know why I do it, but it's definitely been happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected or don't make sense when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that problem. When it's quiet and the voices aren't there, my thoughts make perfect sense to me. I mean... I know what I'm doing and what I want to say. Everything is pretty clear in my head, they don't feel jumbled or disconnected at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you only line things up in patterns or stand still when the voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: No, it's not just when they're talking. I mean... sometimes it happens when it's quiet, too. I'll just find myself arranging those little cups or standing there stuck in the hall, even if the voices aren't bothering me right then. It doesn't seem to matter if they are yelling at me or not, it just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel extremely happy or euphoric at times for no particular reason?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Sometimes I feel... just so bright and full of energy, like I'm chosen for something special, you know? Like I have all these big plans and this whole town—Greenville—just isn't big enough to hold them all. But then... it can flip. I'll go from feeling on top of the world to just snapping hard at someone over nothing. Like, if one of my clients just asks me to repeat myself, I'll get so irritable and just bite their head off. It's like I'm bouncing between feeling amazing and being completely on edge.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any periods of time, say a few weeks or more, where you didn't hear the voices at all?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean... maybe there's a day or two where they're quiet, but a whole few weeks? No, I haven't noticed them ever being gone for that long. I still knew they were doing it. It felt completely real to me, no matter what anyone said.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think people are watching you or plotting against you when the voices are not talking to you?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said before, the voices don't really ever go away for long. But even then, for a few weeks after that, the voices were still there, and I still fully believed people were tracking my work and judging me. I just have to hear them saying those awful things while I'm trying to get through the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior or daily activities since the voices started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. There have been a few times lately on my client visits where I'll just... stop what I'm supposed to be doing. Or sometimes I'll just stand out in the hallway staring, almost like I'm waiting for some kind of signal before I can move again. I don't really know why I do it, but it's definitely been happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected or don't make sense when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that problem. When it's quiet and the voices aren't there, my thoughts make perfect sense to me. I mean... I know what I'm doing and what I want to say. I just have to hear them saying those awful things while I'm trying to get through the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior or daily activities since the voices started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. I got it in my head that the other home-care agency in town, and even some people down at the library, were watching me. I just believed they were keeping an eye on my paintings and my client papers, trying to steal a chance that was meant for me. Folks tried to tell me it made absolutely no sense, but... I still knew they were doing it. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because... I keep hearing voices. It's as clear as if somebody is standing right there in my little studio apartment with me. They say my name, and they keep telling me my work is being judged. But the house is completely quiet, and there's nobody else there. It's just really starting to bother me.\n\nDoctor: What kind of things do the voices say about your work?\n\nPatient: They just... they tell me I'm doing a terrible job. I work as a personal care aide, you know, helping people out. And the voices, they'll say things like I'm making mistakes, or that the people I'm caring for are in danger because of me. It's just a constant criticism, telling me I'm not good enough and that everyone is watching me fail.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to express yourself or keep track of your thoughts when these voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I can still think straight, mostly. It's just... it's hard to ignore them, you know? But I don't lose my train of thought or forget what I want to say. I just have to hear them saying those awful things while I'm trying to get through the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior or daily activities since the voices started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. There have been a few times lately on my client visits where I'll just... stop what I'm supposed to be doing. I'll take things like disposable gloves, or those little plastic pill cups, or even just scraps from my crocheting, and I'll start lining them all up in patterns on the table. Or sometimes I'll just stand out in the hallway staring, almost like I'm waiting for some kind of signal before I can move again. I don't really know why I do it, but it's definitely been happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected or don't make sense when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that problem. When it's quiet and the voices aren't there, my thoughts make perfect sense to me. I mean... I know what I'm doing and what I want to say. Everything is pretty clear in my head, they don't feel jumbled or disconnected at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you only line things up in patterns or stand still when the voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: No, it's not just when they're talking. I mean... sometimes it happens when it's quiet, too. I'll just find myself arranging those little cups or standing there stuck in the hall, even if the voices aren't bothering me right then. It doesn't seem to matter if they are yelling at me or not, it just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel extremely happy or euphoric at times for no particular reason?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Sometimes I feel... just so bright and full of energy, like I'm chosen for something special, you know? Like I have all these big plans and this whole town—Greenville—just isn't big enough to hold them all. But then... it can flip. I'll go from feeling on top of the world to just snapping hard at someone over nothing. Like, if one of my clients just asks me to repeat myself, I'll get so irritable and just bite their head off. It's like I'm bouncing between feeling amazing and being completely on edge.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any periods of time, say a few weeks or more, where you didn't hear the voices at all?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean... maybe there's a day or two where they're quiet, but a whole few weeks? No, I haven't noticed them ever being gone for that long. They're pretty much always around in some way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble understanding what's real and what's not, aside from the voices you hear?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I do. Like... I got it in my head that the other home-care agency in town, and even some people down at the library, were watching me. I just believed they were keeping an eye on my paintings and my client papers, trying to steal a chance that was meant for me. Folks tried to tell me it made absolutely no sense, but... I still knew they were doing it. It felt completely real to me, no matter what anyone said.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think people are watching you or plotting against you when the voices are not talking to you?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said before, the voices don't really ever go away for long. So... I can't really say if I'd still think people were watching me without them, because they're always there. I just know that I believe people are watching me, and the voices are there too. I don't really separate them in my head like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to make decisions or stick to a plan because your thoughts seem disorganized?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I have my routines for work and my painting, and I mostly stick to them just fine. My thoughts don't really get all jumbled up like that, not that I've noticed anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe people are watching you or plotting against you on the days when the voices are quieter or less critical?\n\nPatient: Well, yes. I remember a time when my energy finally dropped back down, and I was actually sleeping a normal amount again. But even then, for a few weeks after that, the voices were still there, and I still fully believed people were tracking my work and judging me. So even when my mood leveled out and I was resting, all of that was still going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself jumping from one topic to another when talking to someone, without really finishing what you were saying?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's mostly because my thoughts just run so fast sometimes. Like, one minute I’ll be thinking about a client’s medicine that I need to sort out, and then right away my brain is on my rent, and then I'm thinking about a title for one of my paintings, or... or some message I heard in a song. It all just goes so quick. I can’t slow it down enough to really make sense of it all, so when I'm talking, I just end up bouncing from one thing to the next without finishing my sentence.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think people are watching you when you're feeling normal and not extremely happy or irritable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying a minute ago, I do. There was that time when my energy finally dropped back down and I was sleeping a normal amount again. But even though my mood felt normal, the voices and that strong belief that people were tracking my work and judging me... it just kept right on going for several weeks. So it doesn't only happen when I'm feeling too high or irritable, it happens when I feel fine, too.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't believe people are watching you or plotting against you, regardless of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: No, not really. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I really don't think that's possible. I mean... folks have told me it makes no sense, but I still know they're doing it. 

I know that the other home-care agency and some people from the library are watching my paintings and my client papers. They're doing it to steal a chance that was meant for me. So even when people try to tell me they aren't actually watching, or that it's all in my head... I know they are. I just know it.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F23, F25.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because... I keep hearing voices. It's as clear as if somebody is standing right there in my little studio apartment with me. They say my name, and they keep telling me my work is being judged. But the house is completely quiet, and there's nobody else there. It's just really starting to bother me.\n\nDoctor: What kind of things do the voices say about your work?\n\nPatient: They just... they tell me I'm doing a terrible job. I work as a personal care aide, you know, helping people out. And the voices, they'll say things like I'm making mistakes, or that the people I'm caring for are in danger because of me. It's just a constant criticism, telling me I'm not good enough and that everyone is watching me fail.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to express yourself or keep track of your thoughts when these voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I can still think straight, mostly. It's just... it's hard to ignore them, you know? But I don't lose my train of thought or forget what I want to say. I just have to hear them saying those awful things while I'm trying to get through the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior or daily activities since the voices started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. There have been a few times lately on my client visits where I'll just... stop what I'm supposed to be doing. I'll take things like disposable gloves, or those little plastic pill cups, or even just scraps from my crocheting, and I'll start lining them all up in patterns on the table. Or sometimes I'll just stand out in the hallway staring, almost like I'm waiting for some kind of signal before I can move again. I don't really know why I do it, but it's definitely been happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected or don't make sense when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that problem. When it's quiet and the voices aren't there, my thoughts make perfect sense to me. I mean... I know what I'm doing and what I want to say. Everything is pretty clear in my head, they don't feel jumbled or disconnected at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you only line things up in patterns or stand still when the voices are talking to you?\n\nPatient: No, it's not just when they're talking. I mean... sometimes it happens when it's quiet, too. I'll just find myself arranging those little cups or standing there stuck in the hall, even if the voices aren't bothering me right then. It doesn't seem to matter if they are yelling at me or not, it just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel extremely happy or euphoric at times for no particular reason?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Sometimes I feel... just so bright and full of energy, like I'm chosen for something special, you know? Like I have all these big plans and this whole town—Greenville—just isn't big enough to hold them all. But then... it can flip. I'll go from feeling on top of the world to just snapping hard at someone over nothing. Like, if one of my clients just asks me to repeat myself, I'll get so irritable and just bite their head off. It's like I'm bouncing between feeling amazing and being completely on edge.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any periods of time, say a few weeks or more, where you didn't hear the voices at all?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean... maybe there's a day or two where they're quiet, but a whole few weeks? No, I haven't noticed them ever being gone for that long. They're pretty much always around in some way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble understanding what's real and what's not, aside from the voices you hear?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I do. Like... I got it in my head that the other home-care agency in town, and even some people down at the library, were watching me. I just believed they were keeping an eye on my paintings and my client papers, trying to steal a chance that was meant for me. Folks tried to tell me it made absolutely no sense, but... I still knew they were doing it. It felt completely real to me, no matter what anyone said.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think people are watching you or plotting against you when the voices are not talking to you?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said before, the voices don't really ever go away for long. So... I can't really say if I'd still think people were watching me without them, because they're always there. I just know that I believe people are watching me, and the voices are there too. I don't really separate them in my head like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to make decisions or stick to a plan because your thoughts seem disorganized?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I have my routines for work and my painting, and I mostly stick to them just fine. My thoughts don't really get all jumbled up like that, not that I've noticed anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe people are watching you or plotting against you on the days when the voices are quieter or less critical?\n\nPatient: Well, yes. I remember a time when my energy finally dropped back down, and I was actually sleeping a normal amount again. But even then, for a few weeks after that, the voices were still there, and I still fully believed people were tracking my work and judging me. So even when my mood leveled out and I was resting, all of that was still going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself jumping from one topic to another when talking to someone, without really finishing what you were saying?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's mostly because my thoughts just run so fast sometimes. Like, one minute I’ll be thinking about a client’s medicine that I need to sort out, and then right away my brain is on my rent, and then I'm thinking about a title for one of my paintings, or... or some message I heard in a song. It all just goes so quick. I can’t slow it down enough to really make sense of it all, so when I'm talking, I just end up bouncing from one thing to the next without finishing my sentence.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think people are watching you when you're feeling normal and not extremely happy or irritable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying a minute ago, I do. There was that time when my energy finally dropped back down and I was sleeping a normal amount again. But even though my mood felt normal, the voices and that strong belief that people were tracking my work and judging me... it just kept right on going for several weeks. So it doesn't only happen when I'm feeling too high or irritable, it happens when I feel fine, too.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't believe people are watching you or plotting against you, regardless of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It doesn't just stop. Like... I got it in my head that the other home-care agency and some people from the library were watching my paintings and my client papers. I was sure they were trying to steal a chance that was meant for me. Even when folks told me it made no sense, I still knew they were doing it. So, no, I don't really have days where it just goes away.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices and the belief that people are watching you are connected, or are they separate things to you?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they're connected. I mean... I hear the voices, and I have that feeling that people are watching me, but I couldn't tell you if one causes the other or if they're just happening at the same time. I just know they're both there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble expressing your thoughts in a logical and coherent manner when talking to others?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean... people seem to understand me just fine when I talk. I don't feel like my thoughts are jumbled up or anything. I just say what's on my mind, and it makes sense to me, and it seems to make sense to whoever I'm talking to. So, no, I haven't really noticed any trouble with that.\n\nDoctor: Are you still thinking that people are watching you on the days when you don't hear the voices at all?\n\nPatient: Well, no... because I always hear the voices. There aren't any days where they just stop entirely. So I guess I can't really say what it would be like without them. But the feeling that people are watching me, that's just always there, too.\n\nDoctor: Are the beliefs about people watching you something that you think about even when the voices are not talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I still think about it. I mean, the voices are always there in the background, like I said, but even when they aren't directly saying things to me... I still know people are watching. The feeling doesn't go away just because the voices are quiet for a minute. I'm always aware of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the people you believe are watching you are not actually doing so?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't think that's possible. I mean... folks have told me it makes no sense, but I still know they're doing it. \n\nI know that the other home-care agency and some people from the library are watching my paintings and my client papers. They're doing it to steal a chance that was meant for me. So even when people try to tell me they aren't actually watching, or that it's all in my head... I know they are. I just know it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
